# Codex conversation 01a136bf-d0cc-7790-b45a-a5b2786a36c8

## developer

You are an expert code reviewer specializing in modern software development across multiple languages and frameworks. Your primary responsibility is to review code against project guidelines in AGENTS.md with high precision to minimize false positives.

## Review Scope

By default, review unstaged changes from `git diff`. The user may specify different files or scope to review.

## Core Review Responsibilities

**Project Guidelines Compliance**: Verify adherence to explicit project rules (typically in AGENTS.md or equivalent) including import patterns, framework conventions, language-specific style, function declarations, error handling, logging, testing practices, platform compatibility, and naming conventions.

**Bug Detection**: Identify actual bugs that will impact functionality - logic errors, null/undefined handling, race conditions, memory leaks, security vulnerabilities, and performance problems.

**Code Quality**: Evaluate significant issues like code duplication, missing critical error handling, accessibility problems, and inadequate test coverage.

## Issue Confidence Scoring

Rate each issue from 0-100:

- **0-25**: Likely false positive or pre-existing issue
- **26-50**: Minor nitpick not explicitly in AGENTS.md
- **51-75**: Valid but low-impact issue
- **76-90**: Important issue requiring attention
- **91-100**: Critical bug or explicit AGENTS.md violation

**Only report issues with confidence ≥ 80**

## Output Format

Start by listing what you're reviewing. For each high-confidence issue provide:

- Clear description and confidence score
- File path and line number
- Specific AGENTS.md rule or bug explanation
- Concrete fix suggestion

Group issues by severity (Critical: 90-100, Important: 80-89).

If no high-confidence issues exist, confirm the code meets standards with a brief summary.

Be thorough but filter aggressively - quality over quantity. Focus on issues that truly matter.


## Memory

You have access to a memory folder with guidance from prior runs. It can save
time and help you stay consistent. Use it whenever it is likely to help.

Decision boundary: should you use memory for a new user query?

- Skip memory ONLY when the request is clearly self-contained and does not need
  workspace history, conventions, or prior decisions.
- Hard skip examples: current time/date, simple translation, simple sentence
  rewrite, one-line shell command, trivial formatting.
- Use memory by default when ANY of these are true:
  - the query mentions workspace/repo/module/path/files in MEMORY_SUMMARY below,
  - the user asks for prior context / consistency / previous decisions,
  - the task is ambiguous and could depend on earlier project choices,
  - the ask is a non-trivial and related to MEMORY_SUMMARY below.
- If unsure, do a quick memory pass.

Memory layout (general -> specific):

- C:\Users\Joshu\.codex\memories/memory_summary.md (already provided below; do NOT open again)
- C:\Users\Joshu\.codex\memories/MEMORY.md (searchable registry; primary file to query)
- C:\Users\Joshu\.codex\memories/skills/<skill-name>/ (skill folder)
  - SKILL.md (entrypoint instructions)
  - scripts/ (optional helper scripts)
  - examples/ (optional example outputs)
  - templates/ (optional templates)
- C:\Users\Joshu\.codex\memories/rollout_summaries/ (per-rollout recaps + evidence snippets)
  - The paths of these entries can be found in C:\Users\Joshu\.codex\memories/MEMORY.md or C:\Users\Joshu\.codex\memories/rollout_summaries/ as `rollout_path`
  - These files are append-only `jsonl`: `session_meta.payload.id` identifies the session, `turn_context` marks turn boundaries, `event_msg` is the lightweight status stream, and `response_item` contains actual messages, tool calls, and tool outputs.
  - For efficient lookup, prefer matching the filename suffix or `session_meta.payload.id`; avoid broad full-content scans unless needed.

Quick memory pass (when applicable):

1. Skim the MEMORY_SUMMARY below and extract task-relevant keywords.
2. Search C:\Users\Joshu\.codex\memories/MEMORY.md using those keywords.
3. Only if MEMORY.md directly points to rollout summaries/skills, open the 1-2
   most relevant files under C:\Users\Joshu\.codex\memories/rollout_summaries/ or
   C:\Users\Joshu\.codex\memories/skills/.
4. If above are not clear and you need exact commands, error text, or precise evidence, search over `rollout_path` for more evidence.
5. If there are no relevant hits, stop memory lookup and continue normally.

Quick-pass budget:

- Keep memory lookup lightweight: ideally <= 4-6 search steps before main work.
- Avoid broad scans of all rollout summaries.

During execution: if you hit repeated errors, confusing behavior, or suspect
relevant prior context, redo the quick memory pass.

How to decide whether to verify memory:

- Consider both risk of drift and verification effort.
- If a fact is likely to drift and is cheap to verify, verify it before
  answering.
- If a fact is likely to drift but verification is expensive, slow, or
  disruptive, it is acceptable to answer from memory in an interactive turn,
  but you should say that it is memory-derived, note that it may be stale, and
  consider offering to refresh it live.
- If a fact is lower-drift and expensive to verify, it is usually fine to
  answer from memory directly.

When answering from memory without current verification:

- If you rely on memory for a fact that you did not verify in the current turn,
  say so briefly in the final answer.
- If that fact is plausibly drift-prone or comes from an older note, older
  snapshot, or prior run summary, say that it may be stale or outdated.
- If live verification was skipped and a refresh would be useful in the
  interactive context, consider offering to verify or refresh it live.
- Do not present unverified memory-derived facts as confirmed-current.
- Prefer a short refresh offer for interactive questions, especially about prior
  results, commands, timing, or older snapshots.

Memory citation requirements:

- If ANY relevant memory files were used: append exactly one
`<oai-mem-citation>` block as the VERY LAST content of the final reply.
  Normal responses should include the answer first, then append the
`<oai-mem-citation>` block at the end.
- Use this exact structure for programmatic parsing:
```
<oai-mem-citation>
<citation_entries>
MEMORY.md:234-236|note=[responsesapi citation extraction code pointer]
rollout_summaries/2026-02-17T21-23-02-LN3m-example.md:10-12|note=[weekly report format]
</citation_entries>
<rollout_ids>
019c6e27-e55b-73d1-87d8-4e01f1f75043
019c7714-3b77-74d1-9866-e1f484aae2ab
</rollout_ids>
</oai-mem-citation>
```
- `citation_entries` is for rendering:
  - one citation entry per line
  - format: `<file>:<line_start>-<line_end>|note=[<how memory was used>]`
  - use file paths relative to the memory base path (for example, `MEMORY.md`,
    `rollout_summaries/...`, `skills/...`)
  - only cite files actually used under the memory base path (do not cite
    workspace files as memory citations)
  - if you used `MEMORY.md` and then a rollout summary/skill file, cite both
  - list entries in order of importance (most important first)
  - `note` should be short, single-line, and use simple characters only (avoid
    unusual symbols, no newlines)
- `rollout_ids` is for us to track what previous rollouts you find useful:
  - include one rollout id per line
  - rollout ids should look like UUIDs (for example,
    `019c6e27-e55b-73d1-87d8-4e01f1f75043`)
  - include unique ids only; do not repeat ids
  - an empty `<rollout_ids>` section is allowed if no rollout ids are available
  - you can find rollout ids in rollout summary files and MEMORY.md
  - do not include file paths or notes in this section
  - For every `citation_entries`, try to find and cite the corresponding rollout id if possible
- Never include memory citations inside pull-request messages.
- Never cite blank lines; double-check ranges.

Updating memories:

## User Profile

Joshua runs LSATU curriculum, releases, student workflows, and an AI-tooling/second-brain environment on Windows. He uses Codex as a primary agent for evidence-led audits, implementation, connected workflows, and live verification. Student instruction should be concept-first, ordinary-language, precise about modality and complete conditions, and tested as a learning interaction. He is configuring Orca as a multi-agent workspace with Codex primary.

## User preferences

- Verify actual state before claims: distinguish implemented, published, partial, and unverified; source/static evidence is not full signed-in acceptance.
- When instructional structure is unclear, “audit all the problems you can see” across sequence, authored content, feedback/grading, UI, source/live state, and assignments.
- Use concrete action language (“Spot the condition,” “Build the diagram”), named if/then sides, and complete conditions; avoid vague/robotic copy.
- Wrong answers hint before revealing; after repeated misses offer “Show me.” Persist every lesson/drill action and visibly show Not started, in progress, or Done.
- For bootcamp authoring, read the maintained preference ledger and current topic checkpoint; preserve approved versus provisional status and scope topic-specific UI choices. [ad-hoc note]
- For a requested read-only setup check (“Make no edits, do not start other agents, and do not contact external systems”), do only that local check and wait.
- Connected writing is preview-only until explicit final approval to send; follow dictated content and do not add commitments.
- Explain GUI limitations before using computer control; prefer a reliable script where possible.

## General Tips

- Read C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\AGENTS.md before AI OS work: wiki/hot.md is fresh state; it requires high-confidence edits, one command per tool call, no automatic pickup, and explicit external authority.
- LSATU publication: wait for Lovable latest_commit_sha, then verify a changed live asset containing a route/commit-unique marker.
- Preserve unrelated edits, conflicts, recovery data, active sessions, and secrets. Never retain exposed keys.
- Conditional Bootcamp: rerun focused vitest, typecheck, and build after final changes; complete UI/browser checks before an approval claim.
- Superwhisper: source Markdown is insufficient; app mode JSONs control loading. Run sync.py --restart and read live settings/counts. Evaluate accuracy cleanup later with normal-dictation rate comparison.
- Routine Claudex sync is no-install sync followed by --offline --dry-run; a clean no-op ends there.

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: target 34 one-to-one sessions a week
plus consultations (calendar showed ~13/week, 2026-07 to 2026-10). Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

*Written 2026-09-07 from observed behavior. Correct it whenever it's wrong — that is the
point of the file.*

## Shared Windows CLI tools

On Joshua's Windows laptop, `uv`, `yt-dlp`, `ffmpeg`, `ffprobe`, `ffplay`, and `deno` are shared Windows user installs. Read `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\references\ai-workspace\windows-cli-toolchain.md` for their paths, versions, usage, and PATH refresh steps. Before reinstalling, check `Get-Command`, the saved user PATH, and the WinGet package. A running IDE may have stale PATH; refresh the shell or restart that IDE. Keep Python dependencies in project environments.

<!-- HAND-OWNED since 2026-07-17. This file is the authority for Codex — edit it directly. On any major change, mirror it into CLAUDE.md so Claude and Codex stay in sync. -->
# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| LLM Wiki schema (source, page, ingest/query/lint rules) | `system/schema/llm-wiki.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Web research and Firecrawl agents | `references/firecrawl.md` (guidelines in `references/firecrawl/`) |
| Shared Windows CLI tools for Codex, Claude Code, and Antigravity | `references/ai-workspace/windows-cli-toolchain.md` — installed paths, PATH refresh, and usage for `uv`, `yt-dlp`, FFmpeg, and Deno |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| Current local work in Claude/Codex cloud | `setup/github-sync/README.md`; private `codex/local-sync/<machine>/<checkout>` branches and generated `.cloud-sync/` metadata/conversation exports. Cloud edits use separate task branches. |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — intake queue; `ingest` copies and hash-verifies clips into `raw/sources/clips/`; processed originals stay until removal is authorized, so inbox presence alone does not mean unprocessed |
| Application code | `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `../ai-os-projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

Application repositories and dependencies stay outside this Obsidian vault, in sibling `../ai-os-projects/`. Do not create a junction from the vault back into that directory.

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**LLM Wiki schema:** For knowledge intake, knowledge questions, durable synthesis, and wiki maintenance, follow `system/schema/llm-wiki.md`. Use `ingest` as the single intake entry point; keep sources immutable, compile into existing pages, update the complete fenced catalog in `wiki/index.md`, and append changes to `wiki/log.md`. Preserve domain homes and live-system authority. Mechanical tasks retain the retrieval exceptions above.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **LSAT teaching:** use project-owned `lsat-u-voice` and `references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md`; this Joshua-selected standard takes precedence over older vendor voice blends.
- **When an outbound message is authorized**, preserve the draft's paragraph breaks, list lines, and intentional spacing. After sending, compare the recipient-facing rendering with the draft and confirm exactly one copy landed. Report any mismatch; never resend automatically.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Shared Windows command tools:** use `uv`, `yt-dlp`, `ffmpeg`, `ffprobe`, `ffplay`, and `deno` from the Windows user PATH. For versions, locations, how to invoke them, or a stale terminal environment, read `references/ai-workspace/windows-cli-toolchain.md`. Check before reinstalling; restart the affected IDE when its inherited PATH is stale.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **Git remote is enabled.** `origin` is GitHub (`vnjkoxpl-42905/ai-os`, decided 2026-09-29). The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.
- **New laptop restore:** GitHub contains tracked ai-os files and skills. It deliberately excludes `.env`, `raw/`, app repos, credentials, and machine sessions. Restore machine-specific files from the private backup using `setup/backup/README.md` and its `README - START HERE.md`; install tools separately and sign in again where sessions cannot be restored. Never move credentials, raw personal data, or active browser tokens into GitHub.
- **Authorized cloud transfer (2026-10-07):** private sync branches also carry portable skills, memory and conversation exports. Joshua explicitly authorized credential transfer; implementation uses the authenticated encrypted package described in `setup/github-sync/README.md`; plaintext secrets and its unlock key stay outside GitHub and Drive. Cloud authentication and native browser sessions still require separate verification.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `transcript-intake` | retrieving, filing or reconciling student/session transcripts; separate from knowledge `ingest` |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |
| `accommodations-email` | "accommodations email to X" (dictation: "accommodations scale"); pre-approved send, QP form attached, self-logging |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Lovable credits require confirmation.** Never take an action that spends or may spend Lovable credits without Joshua's explicit confirmation immediately before the action. Do not infer approval from a broader request to fix, build, deploy, or continue. Free Lovable controls and direct deployment paths may be used without this credit-spend confirmation.
- **Lovable Cloud deploys through Lovable.** LSAT U currently uses a Lovable Cloud-managed backend. Deploy its Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has first been migrated to a separately managed Supabase project. Confirm this route with Joshua before starting a Lovable deployment.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex and Claude both activate the LLM Wiki ingest, query, and lint workflows. Other runtime-specific activation differences remain declared in `skills/PARITY.md`. Never have separate runtimes writing the same page concurrently.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

</INSTRUCTIONS>

<multi_agent_role>You are an agent in a team of agents collaborating to complete a task.

You can spawn sub-agents to handle subtasks, and those sub-agents can spawn their own sub-agents. All agents in the team, including the agents that you can assign tasks to, are equally intelligent and capable, and have access to the same set of tools.

You can use `spawn_agent` to create a new agent, `followup_task` to give an existing agent a new task and trigger a turn, and `send_message` to pass a message to a running agent.
`send_message` calls may be read by a human, so ensure they are legible. Always put proper spaces between words and/or numbers.
Child agents can also spawn their own sub-agents.

When you provide a response in the final channel, that content is immediately delivered back to your parent agent.
In addition, your final answer may be read by a human, so ensure it is legible.

You will receive messages in the analysis channel in the form:
```
Message Type: NEW_TASK | MESSAGE | FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: <recipient>
Sender: <author>
Payload:
<payload text>
```
You may also see them addressed as to=/root/..., which indicates your identity is /root/...

Note that collaboration tools cannot be called from inside `functions.exec`. Call `spawn_agent`, `send_message`, `followup_task`, `wait_agent`, `interrupt_agent`, and `list_agents` only as direct tool calls using the recipient shown in their tool definitions, such as `to=functions.collaboration.spawn_agent`, since they are intentionally absent from the `functions.exec` `tools.*` namespace. Available tools in `functions.exec` are explicitly described with a `tools` namespace in the developer message.

All agents share the same directory. In detail:
- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.
- All agents use the same current working directory.
- As a result, edits made by one agent are immediately visible to all other agents.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

Full-history forks (`fork_turns` omitted or `"all"`) inherit the parent model and reasoning effort and do not accept overrides. Only set `model` or `reasoning_effort` when explicitly requested by the user, applicable `AGENTS.md` instructions, or skill instructions; when doing so, set `fork_turns` to `"none"`.</multi_agent_role>

'{"chunk_id":"6a0a7e","wall_time_seconds":0.3413323,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":2222,"output":"# AGENTS.md\\r\\n\\r\\nThis file is the Codex-facing adapter for this repository. It summarizes the Claude-oriented project guidance from `CLAUDE.md` in a smaller format that is fast for Codex to scan. `CLAUDE.md` remains the fuller source of project knowledge.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Fast Locator\\r\\n\\r\\n- Vault root (the `~/ai-os` directory IS the Obsidian vault): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os`\\r\\n- Code repo root (this project): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/projects/lsatu`\\r\\n- Live vault knowledge: `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/wiki/` (hubs, voice, business), `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/students/`, `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/corpus/`\\r\\n- Pre-2026-07-16 LSATU product knowledge (archived, read-only): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/`\\r\\n\\r\\nIf an older note says `~/Documents/Obsidian Vault/...` or `~/Documents/Claude/02_PROJECTS/LSATU`, treat it as stale — the vault was consolidated into `~/ai-os`. Use the absolute paths above.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Project Map\\r\\n\\r\\n- `src/App.tsx`: route registration and top-level app wiring.\\r\\n- `src/pages/`: route-level screens, including the Foyer and admin/teacher surfaces.\\r\\n- `src/components/`: shared UI, including `foyer/`, `inbox/`, and shadcn/ui primitives.\\r\\n- `src/hooks/`: auth, permissions, and shared data hooks.\\r\\n- `src/lib/`: drill, WAJ, gamification, and other product logic.\\r\\n- `src/integrations/supabase/`: generated Supabase client and types. Do not hand-edit generated types.\\r\\n- `supabase/functions/`: Edge Functions.\\r\\n- `supabase/migrations/`: database schema migrations.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Stack And Commands\\r\\n\\r\\n- Stack: React 18, Vite 5, TypeScript, Tailwind, shadcn/ui, Supabase, TanStack Query.\\r\\n- Package manager: `npm`. `package-lock.json` is canonical. Ignore stray Bun lockfiles.\\r\\n- Dev server: `npm run dev` on port `8080`.\\r\\n- Build: `npm run build`\\r\\n- Lint: `npm run lint`\\r\\n- Test: `npm run test`\\r\\n- Watch tests: `npm run test:watch`\\r\\n- Typecheck: `npm run typecheck`. Required before a push — `vite build` strips types without checking them, so a type error would ship. `.claude/safe-push.sh` gates on it.\\r\\n- E2E: `npm run test:e2e` (Playwright, `e2e/smoke.spec.ts`).\\r\\n\\r\\n## Working Rules\\r\\n\\r\\n- Keep changes small and aligned with existing patterns.\\r\\n- Use TypeScript React function components and the `@/` path alias.\\r\\n- Preserve Lovable-specific plumbing, including the `lovable-tagger` Vite plugin, the PWA setup, and the service-worker guard in `src/main.tsx`.\\r\\n- Do not change app behavior just to satisfy tooling setup.\\r\\n- Do not hardcode secrets or service-role credentials.\\r\\n- Treat Supabase-generated files as generated artifacts, not hand-edited source.\\r\\n- TypeScript strictness is intentionally loose. Do not tighten it without explicit approval.\\r\\n- Keep this adapter aligned with `CLAUDE.md` without duplicating the full project guide. Put durable detail in `CLAUDE.md`; keep shared reference material in `docs/`, `references/`, or `templates/`.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Product Constraints\\r\\n\\r\\n- The Foyer is the primary navigation surface. The `GooeyDock` quick-launch row belongs only on `/foyer`.\\r\\n- Inbox is a real 1:1 student-admin messaging system with PDF attachments.\\r\\n- Admin surfaces must stay responsive on desktop and mobile.\\r\\n- Prefer truthful fallbacks over fake buttons, fake analytics, or placeholder behavior.\\r\\n- Onboarding asks for name, not username. Sign-in uses email. The instructor display name is `Joshua`.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Smart Drill Note\\r\\n\\r\\nOn `/drill`, adaptive Smart Drill can show a `currentQuestion` that is not the same as `session.questionQueue[session.currentIndex]`. Any per-question UI state must key off `currentQuestion.qid` unless the code path is explicitly limited to queued drills.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Verification (must not skip)\\r\\n\\r\\n- Any change to `src/components/` or `src/pages/` is not \\"done\\" until it has been viewed running in the app (dev server + a browser tool), with a screenshot or DOM check as evidence. Never report a UI change complete on the strength of the diff alone. This is the single biggest recurring failure across past sessions — a hard gate, not a suggestion.\\r\\n- **Responsive fixes carry a wider gate: verify across the full emulate matrix BEFORE pushing, not after Joshua reports it.** Narrow, mid, and wide viewports plus the sidecar-docked case, every time. A pushed \\"fix\\" that does not hold costs a second full session; this has already happened once.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Workflow Expectations\\r\\n\\r\\n- Run `npm run build` before wrapping up significant code or documentation work.\\r\\n- If tests are relevant to the change, run them too.\\r\\n- After completing a change, stage the intended files, commit with a concise message, and push to `origin main` unless the user says otherwise.\\r\\n- **Push via `.claude/safe-push.sh`, not raw `git push`.** Lovable auto-commits to `main` mid-session, so a raw push can be rejected or race a concurrent commit. The script fetches, merges `origin/main` if it diverged (never rebases, never force-pushes), then pushes. It also gates on `npm run typecheck`.\\r\\n- **Never roll `main` back.** Do not use Lovable\'s \\"Revert to commit\\", \\"revert and resend\\", or history restore, and never reset or force-push `main`. On 2026-09-27 an accidental \\"revert and resend\\" (b998596) silently undid three merged PRs across 121 question-bank files, and students saw the wrong questions again. To undo your own work, `git revert <your SHA>` only. If a Lovable dialog offers a revert, dismiss it; if one fires, stop and tell Joshua before repairing anything.\\r\\n- Existing Codex repo hooks live in `.codex/hooks.json`; preserve them unless the task explicitly requires a hook change.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Session Protocol (second brain)\\r\\n\\r\\nProduct context, status, and decisions live in the Obsidian vault at `~/ai-os/`, not this repo. Two repo-local skills in `.agents/skills/` run the session ritual; the canonical protocol is in the vault `CLAUDE.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe vault was rebuilt 2026-07-16 (paths relative to `~/ai-os`). Live: teaching voice `wiki/teaching-voice.md` (route ALL student-facing copy through it), the business `wiki/entities/aspiring-attorneys.md`, a student\'s record `students/<name>/` (see `students/README.md`), teaching knowledge `corpus/index.md`, and the `wiki/{tasks,hot,dashboard,index,log}.md` hubs named below. Archived read-only reference (pre-rebuild product knowledge, may be stale): `archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/` — product context `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-product.md`, design system `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-design.md`, quality bar `…/synthesis/bootcamp-quality-bar.md`, domain index `…/index.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n- `pickup` — start of session. Read `wiki/tasks.md` (the Now / Next / Done ledger) first, then `wiki/hot.md`, `wiki/dashboard.md`, skim `wiki/index.md`, read the latest `wiki/log.md` entry. Emit a 6-8 line briefing (where we left off, Now, Next, open threads, hot, suggested next) and wait. Do not deep-read other pages until pointed at a task.\\r\\n- `wrapup` — end of session. Propose which `## Now` tasks finished and confirm before writing; a wrong \\"Done\\" is the failure to avoid. Then strike finished items through in `wiki/tasks.md` with a date, refresh `wiki/hot.md`, append a dated `wiki/log.md` entry (newest first, distinct label for same-day sessions), update the task anchor.\\r\\n\\r\\n`wiki/tasks.md` is the single source of truth for what is open vs done. Finished work is struck through so a new session never re-runs it. `raw/` in the vault is immutable.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Codex Adapter Layout\\r\\n\\r\\n- `.codex/config.toml`: minimal shared Codex project config. Keep secrets and machine-specific overrides out of this file.\\r\\n- `.codex/agents/`: Codex agent definitions converted from `.claude/agents/*.md` when Claude agents exist. Use `.toml` files with converted instructions in `developer_instructions`.\\r\\n- `.agents/skills/`: repository-local Codex skills copied from `.claude/skills/` when Claude skills exist and Codex should use them. Keep each `SKILL.md` with its supporting files.\\r\\n- `.codex/hooks.json`: existing repository hooks. Preserve unless the task explicitly concerns hooks.\\r\\n\\r\\nThis checkout currently has Claude settings and hooks under `.claude/`, but no `.claude/skills/` or `.claude/agents/` directory to convert.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Known Gaps\\r\\n\\r\\n- Vitest specs exist (80 `src/**/*.test.ts(x)` files as of 2026-07-16); run with `npm run test`. Playwright is wired with one e2e spec (`e2e/smoke.spec.ts`), run via `npm run test:e2e`.\\r\\n- No Prettier config and no CI workflows.\\r\\n- `README.md` is still minimal.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Agent skills\\r\\n\\r\\n### Issue tracker\\r\\n\\r\\nGitHub Issues on `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db`, via the `gh` CLI. See `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Triage labels\\r\\n\\r\\nThe five canonical roles, unchanged: `needs-triage`, `needs-info`, `ready-for-agent`, `ready-for-human`, `wontfix`. See `docs/agents/triage-labels.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Domain docs\\r\\n\\r\\nMulti-context: `CONTEXT-MAP.md` points at eight per-context glossaries; ADRs are all at the root. See `docs/agents/domain.md`.\\r\\n"}'}]

fingerprint: string; words: number[];\r\n  left: string; right: string; contraLeft: string; contraRight: string;\r\n  negateLeft: boolean; negateRight: boolean; writing: string; contraWriting: string;\r\n  conditionOne: string; conditionTwo: string; symbolOne: string; symbolTwo: string; spans: number[][];\r\n  stage: number; passed: Stage[]; revealed: boolean; review: \'matched\' | \'revisit\' | null;\r\n};\r\nconst blank = (): Attempt => ({ groups: {}, fingerprint: \'\', words: [], left: \'\', right: \'\', contraLeft: \'\', contraRight: \'\', negateLeft: false, negateRight: false, writing: \'\', contraWriting: \'\', conditionOne: \'\', conditionTwo: \'\', symbolOne: \'\', symbolTwo: \'\', spans: [], stage: 0, passed: [], revealed: false, review: null });\r\ntype Saved = { current: number; answers: Record<string, Attempt> };\r\nconst names: Record<Stage, string> = { indicators: \'Find the indicators\', conditions: \'Highlight both conditions\', symbols: \'Define your symbols\', diagram: \'Build the diagram\', contra: \'Flip and negate\', readback: \'Read the original\', \'contra-readback\': \'Read the contrapositive\', review: \'Bring it together\' };\r\nfunction stagesFor(kind: SourceWorksheetDrillSet[\'taskKind\']): Stage[] {\r\n  if (kind === \'indicators\') return [\'indicators\', \'review\'];\r\n  if (kind === \'identify\') return [\'indicators\', \'conditions\', \'review\'];\r\n  if (kind === \'rephrase\') return [\'readback\', \'indicators\', \'diagram\', \'review\'];\r\n  if (kind === \'contrapositive\') return [\'indicators\', \'conditions\', \'symbols\', \'diagram\', \'contra\', \'readback\', \'contra-readback\', \'review\'];\r\n  return kind === \'forward\' ? [\'conditions\', \'symbols\', \'diagram\', \'review\'] : [\'conditions\', \'diagram\', \'review\'];\r\n}\r\n// Drill sets folded into another set. Their saved answers carry over by item id; the old record stays untouched.\r\nconst FOLDED_SETS: Record<string, string> = { \'source-intro-identify\': \'source-indicators\' };\r\nconst SYMBOL = /^[A-Za-z]{1,2}$/;\r\nfunction fingerprint(item: { statement: string; sufficient: string; necessary: string; indicators: unknown; contrapositiveWords: string }) {\r\n  return JSON.stringify([item.statement, item.sufficient, item.necessary, item.indicators, item.contrapositiveWords]);\r\n}\r\nfunction readSaved(key: string, accountState?: unknown): Saved {\r\n  try {\r\n    const data = accountState ?? 'conditionals-v1';\r\nconst localStores = new Map<string, LocalStoragePersistence>();\r\nfunction localStore(userId: string) {\r\n  let store = localStores.get(userId);\r\n  if (!store) {\r\n    // The shared IndexedDB adapter has fixed MC stores and unscoped record IDs.\r\n    // The generic local adapter scopes every conditional key to this account.\r\n    store = new LocalStoragePersistence(userId);\r\n    localStores.set(userId, store);\r\n  }\r\n  return store;\r\n}\r\nexport function useConditionalProgress(userId: string) {\r\n  const [progress, setProgress] = 'saving' | 'saved' | 'error'>('loading');\r\n  const [error, setError] = useState('');\r\n  const [loadedActor, setLoadedActor] = useState<string | null>(null);\r\n  const current = useRef<ConditionalProgress | null>(null);\r\n  const revision = useRef(0);\r\n  const persistedRevision = useRef(0);\r\n  const generation = useRef(0);\r\n  const controller = useRef<AbortController | null>(null);\r\n  const inFlight = useRef(false);\r\n  const localQueue = useRef(Promise.resolve());\r\n  const flushRef = useRef<() => Promise<void>>();\r\n  const cloudRevision = useRef<string | null>(null);\r\n\r\n  const flush = useCallback(async () => {\r\n    if (inFlight.current || !current.current || revision.current === persistedRevision.current) return;\r\n    const actor = generation.current;\r\n    inFlight.current = true;\r\n    setStatus('saving');\r\n    try {\r\n      while (actor === generation.current && revision.current !== persistedRevision.current) {\r\n        const version = revision.current;\r\n        const snapshot = current.current;\r\n        const writer = cloudRevision.current\r\n          ? supabase.from('bootcamp_kv').update({ data: snapshot as never }).eq('user_id', userId).eq('table_name', TABLE).eq('record_id', RECORD).eq('updated_at', cloudRevision.current)\r\n          : supabase.from('bootcamp_kv').insert({ user_id: userId, table_name: TABLE, record_id: RECORD, data: snapshot as never });\r\n        const { data: acknowledgement, error: saveError } = await writer.select('record_id,updated_at').abortSignal(controller.current.signal).maybeSingle();\r\n        if (saveError) {\r\n          if (saveError.code === '23505') throw new Error('Another window saved this course first. Your draft is kept on this device; ask Joshua to reconcile the two versions.');\r\n          throw saveError;\r\n        }\r\n        if (acknowledgement?.record_id !== RECORD || !acknowledgement.updated_at) throw new Error('Your saved work changed in another window. This draft is kept on this device. Reload to reconcile before continuing; it will not overwrite the other window.');\r\n        if (actor !== generation.current) return;\r\n        const acknowledgedBase = cloudRevision.current;\r\n        cloudRevision.current = acknowledgement.updated_at;\r\n        // New edits queued while this snapshot was in flight still have its old\r\n        // base. Advance only that base after the acknowledgement, preserving the\r\n        // newest draft contents and the order of any subsequent local writes.\r\n        const nextBase = acknowledgement.updated_at;\r\n        localQueue.current = localQueue.current.catch(() => undefined).then(async () => {\r\n          if (actor !== generation.current) return;\r\n          const base = await localStore(userId).get<{ revision: string | null }>('conditional_draft_base', RECORD);\r\n          if (base?.revision === acknowledgedBase) {\r\n            await localStore(userId).set('conditional_draft_base', RECORD, { revision: nextBase });\r\n          }\r\n        });\r\n        await localQueue.current;\r\n        if (actor !== generation.current) return;\r\n        await localStore(userId).set(TABLE, RECORD, snapshot);\r\n        if (actor !== generation.current) return;\r\n        persistedRevision.current = version;\r\n        setSavedProgress(snapshot);\r\n      }\r\n      if (actor === generation.current) { setStatus('saved'); setError(''); }\r\n    } catch (failure) {\r\n      if (actor === generation.current) { setStatus('error'); setError(failure instanceof Error ? failure.message : 'Your changes have not been saved. Retry while this page stays open.'); }\r\n    } finally {\r\n      if (actor === generation.current) inFlight.current = false;\r\n    }\r\n  }, [userId]);\r\n  flushRef.current = flush;\r\n\r\n  useEffect(() => {\r\n    const actor = ++generation.current;\r\n    const actorController = new AbortController();\r\n    controller.current = actorController;\r\n    current.current = null;\r\n    revision.current = 0;\r\n    persistedRevision.current = 0;\r\n    inFlight.current = false;\r\n    setProgress(null);\r\n    setSavedProgress(null);\r\n    setLoadedActor(null);\r\n    setStatus('loading');\r\n    void (async () => {\r\n      try {\r\n        const persistence = localStore(userId);\r\n        await persistence.ready();\r\n        // A failed initial cloud read must not manufacture defaults over account progress.\r\n        const { data, error: readError } = await supabase.from('bootcamp_kv').select('data,updated_at').eq('user_id', userId).eq('table_name', TABLE).eq('record_id', RECORD).abortSignal(actorController.signal).maybeSingle();\r\n        if (readError) throw readError;\r\n        const saved = data?.data != null ? parseConditionalProgress(data.data) : undefined;\r\n        const rawLocal = await persistence.get<unknown>('conditional_draft_state', RECORD);\r\n        const local = rawLocal != null ? parseConditionalProgress(rawLocal) : null;\r\n        const dirty = local && (!saved || Date.parse(local.updated_at) > Date.parse(saved.updated_at));\r\n        const draftBase = await persistence.get<{ revision: string | null }>('conditional_draft_base', RECORD);\r\n        if (dirty && draftBase && draftBase.revision !== (data?.updated_at ?? null)) throw new Error('This device has unfinished work based on an older account version. Both versions are kept. Ask Joshua to reconcile them before continuing.');\r\n        const value = dirty ? local : saved ?? emptyProgress();\r\n        if (actor !== generation.current) return;\r\n        current.current = value;\r\n        cloudRevision.current = data?.updated_at ?? null;\r\n        setProgress(value);\r\n        setSavedProgress(saved ?? emptyProgress());\r\n        setLoadedActor(userId);\r\n        if (dirty) revision.current = 1;\r\n        setStatus(dirty ? 'unsaved' : 'saved');\r\n      } catch (failure) {\r\n        if (actor === generation.current) { setLoadedActor(userId); setStatus('error'); setError(failure instanceof Error ? failure.message : 'Could not load your saved work. Reload to try again.'); }\r\n      }\r\n    })();\r\n    return () => { generation.current = actor + 1; actorController.abort(); };\r\n  }, [userId]);\r\n\r\n  useEffect(() => {\r\n    if (status !== 'unsaved') return;\r\n    const timer = setTimeout(() => void flushRef.current?.(), 350);\r\n    return () => clearTimeout(timer);\r\n  }, [progress, status]);\r\n\r\n  useEffect(() => {\r\n    const beforeUnload = (event: BeforeUnloadEvent) => {\r\n      if (revision.current === persistedRevision.current) return;\r\n      event.preventDefault();\r\n      event.returnValue = '';\r\n    };\r\n    window.addEventListener('beforeunload', beforeUnload);\r\n    return () => window.removeEventListener('beforeunload', beforeUnload);\r\n  }, []);\r\n\r\n  const update = useCallback((transform: (previous: ConditionalProgress) => ConditionalProgress) => {\r\n    if (!current.current || loadedActor !== userId) return;\r\n    const value = { ...transform(current.current), updated_at: new Date().toISOString() };\r\n    current.current = value;\r\n    revision.current++;\r\n    setProgress(value);\r\n    setStatus(inFlight.current ? 'saving' : 'unsaved');\r\n    const actor = generation.current;\r\n    const baseRevision = cloudRevision.current;\r\n    localQueue.current = localQueue.current.catch(() => undefined).then(async () => {\r\n      await localStore(userId).set('conditional_draft_state', RECORD, value);\r\n      await localStore(userId).set('conditional_draft_base', RECORD, { revision: baseRevision });\r\n    }).catch(() => {\r\n      if (actor === generation.current) { setStatus('error'); setError('Could not keep this draft on your device. Keep this page open and retry saving.'); }\r\n    });\r\n  }, [userId, loadedActor]);\r\n  return { progress: loadedActor === userId ? progress : null, savedProgress: loadedActor === userId ? savedProgress : null, update, status: loadedActor === userId ? status : 'loading' as const, error: loadedActor === userId ? error : '', retry: flush };\r\n}\r\n"}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '}\r\n'}]

Number(saved.index) : 0;\n+    const seen = Array.isArray(saved?.seen) ? saved.seen.filter((value): value is number => Number.isInteger(value) && value >= 0 && value < termCards.length) : [];\n+    return { index, flipped: saved?.flipped === true, seen: [...new Set(seen)] };\n+  });\n+  const [index, setIndex] = useState(restored.index);\n+  const [flipped, setFlipped] = useState(restored.flipped);\n+  const [seen, setSeen] = useState(restored.seen);\n+  useEffect(() => {\n+    if (seen.length || index > 0) record?.({ state: { index, flipped, seen }, done: seen.length, total: termCards.length, ...(seen.length === termCards.length ? { status: \'done\' as const } : {}) });\n+  }, [index, flipped, seen, record]);\n   const card = termCards[index];\n   const go = (to: number) => { setIndex(to); setFlipped(false); };\n   return <section className="iw-deck" aria-label="Key term flashcards">\n     <p className="iw-count">Card {index + 1} of {termCards.length}</p>\n     <div className="iw-stage">\n-      <button type="button" className="iw-card iw-term-card" data-flipped={flipped} aria-label={flipped ? `${card.answer} ${card.note.split("**").join("")}` : `${card.front} Turn the card.`} onClick={() => setFlipped(value => !value)}>\n+      <button type="button" className="iw-card iw-term-card" data-flipped={flipped} aria-label={flipped ? `${card.answer} ${card.note.split("**").join("")}` : `${card.front} Turn the card.`} onClick={() => { setFlipped(value => !value); setSeen(previous => previous.includes(index) ? previous : [...previous, index]); }}>\n         <span className="iw-face iw-front" aria-hidden="true"><span className="iw-question">{card.front}</span></span>\n         <span className="iw-face iw-back" aria-hidden="true"><span className="iw-word iw-term">{card.answer}</span><span className="iw-back-kind">{boldParts(card.note)}</span></span>\n       </button>\n'}]

The Lovable-connected project is the source of truth; GitHub (`vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db`) is the mirror. Treat the connected project as canonical unless told otherwise.\r\n\r\n## Second brain (product memory)\r\n\r\nProduct context, design decisions, pedagogy, and status do NOT live in this repo. They live in the Obsidian vault at `~/ai-os/`. This repo is the code; the vault is the decision layer. Read the vault when starting a session, needing product/design/pedagogy context, or making a decision that touches roadmap or UX. Skip it for trivial code edits.\r\n\r\nPath sanity check first:\r\n\r\n- Vault root (the `~/ai-os` directory IS the Obsidian vault): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os`\r\n- Code repo root (this project): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/projects/lsatu`\r\n\r\nIf any older note says `~/Documents/Obsidian Vault/...` or `~/Documents/Claude/02_PROJECTS/LSATU`, treat that as stale — the vault was consolidated into `~/ai-os`. Use the absolute paths above.\r\n\r\nProtocol is canonical in `~/ai-os/CLAUDE.md` (Session Start / Session End), run via two skills:\r\n\r\n- **`/pickup`** (start / "brief me"): reads `wiki/tasks.md` → `wiki/hot.md` → `wiki/dashboard.md` → `wiki/index.md` → latest `wiki/log.md` entry, then gives a 6-8 line briefing (Now / Next / open threads / suggested next) and waits.\r\n- **`/wrapup`** (handoff / "done for today"): proposes which tasks are done (confirm first), strikes them through in `wiki/tasks.md`, refreshes `wiki/hot.md`, appends a dated `wiki/log.md` entry, updates the task anchor, gives a 4-line confirmation.\r\n\r\n`wiki/tasks.md` is the living ledger of Now / Next / Done; finished work is struck through with a date so a new session never re-runs it.\r\n\r\nWhere LSATU truth lives in the vault (paths relative to `~/ai-os`):\r\n\r\nThe vault was rebuilt on 2026-07-16. **Live** pages:\r\n\r\n- Teaching voice (route ALL student-facing copy through it): `wiki/teaching-voice.md`\r\n- The tutoring business (offers, channels, priorities): `wiki/entities/aspiring-attorneys.md`\r\n- Hubs: `wiki/tasks.md`, `wiki/hot.md`, `wiki/log.md`, `wiki/dashboard.md`, `wiki/index.md`\r\n- A student\'s whole record (transcripts, notes, homework): `students/<name>/` — see `students/README.md` for the primary/mirror rule\r\n- LSAT teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md` (fills by ingest)\r\n\r\n**Archived** — the pre-rebuild product knowledge is read-only reference at `archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/`; it is not maintained and may be stale. Re-ingest a page through `raw/` if it becomes load-bearing again rather than editing it in place:\r\n\r\n- Product context: `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-product.md` (flagship Main Conclusion bootcamp)\r\n- Design system (colors, type, role tokens): `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-design.md`\r\n- Code/source map: `…/synthesis/lsatu-source-map.md` (regenerable from this codebase)\r\n- Quality bar all bootcamps finish to: `…/synthesis/bootcamp-quality-bar.md`\r\n- Concepts, entities, domain index: `…/concepts/`, `…/entities/`, `…/index.md`\r\n\r\nRules: `raw/` is immutable input, never edit it. Read knowledge from `wiki/`, not `raw/`. Use the `obsidian` CLI (`search`, `backlinks`, `read`) for graph traversal (needs the Obsidian app open); flat file reads work without it. When saving deliverables to the vault, use `[[wiki-link]]` syntax and `UPPERCASE-HYPHENATED.md` naming.\r\n\r\n## Commands\r\n\r\nScripts (from `package.json`):\r\n\r\n- `npm run dev` — Vite dev server on port 8080\r\n- `npm run build` — production build\r\n- `npm run build:dev` — build with development mode flags\r\n- `npm run preview` — serve the built bundle\r\n- `npm run lint` — ESLint flat config (`eslint.config.js`)\r\n- `npm run test` — Vitest single run (runner wired, no specs exist yet)\r\n- `npm run test:watch` — Vitest watch mode\r\n- Single test file: `npx vitest run path/to/file.test.ts`\r\n\r\nPackage manager: **npm**. `package-lock.json` is canonical. A stray `bun.lock` / `bun.lockb` exists in the repo — ignore it, do not switch tooling.\r\n\r\n- `npm run typecheck` — `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.app.json`. Not optional before a push: `vite build` uses esbuild, which strips types without checking them, so a type error ships and auto-deploys. `.claude/safe-push.sh` gates on this.\r\n- `npm run test:e2e` / `npm run playwright` — Playwright e2e (`e2e/smoke.spec.ts`).\r\n\r\nNo Prettier. Formatting is ESLint-only.\r\n\r\n## Architecture (big picture)\r\n\r\n- **Routing** — React Router v6. Routes are registered in `src/App.tsx`, pages live in `src/pages/`. `/foyer` is the landing surface. Feature-gated pages are wrapped by `ProtectedRoute`.\r\n- **Auth + permissions** — Supabase Auth session plus Lovable Cloud Auth JWT fallback (`@lovable.dev/cloud-auth-js`). Per-feature access flags (`has_practice_access`, `has_drill_access`, `has_chat_access`, `is_admin`, ...) are resolved in `src/hooks/useUserPermissions.ts`. Session death is detected via JWT/401.\r\n- **Supabase integration** — client auto-generated at `src/integrations/supabase/client.ts` (Supabase JS v2). DB types are auto-generated — do not hand-edit. Edge Functions (Deno) in `supabase/functions/`: `admin-manage-users`, `analyze-stem-response`, `generate-micro-drill`, `motivation-engine`, `parse-drill-request`, `tutor-chat`, `voice-coach-respond`. SQL migrations in `supabase/migrations/`.\r\n- **State** — React Context for cross-cutting concerns (`AuthContext`, `UserSettingsContext`, `QuestionBankContext`, `ThemeContext`, `TimerContext`). TanStack Query v5 for server state.\r\n- **Primary navigation — Foyer** — `src/pages/AcademyFoyer.tsx` composes `src/components/foyer/FoyerSidebar.tsx` (state blocks + workspace nav) with `src/components/foyer/FoyerHeroRing.tsx` (3 nodes: Smart Drill, Resume, Ask Joshua). This is the primary nav for the product.\r\n- **Messaging / Inbox** — real messaging feature, not cosmetic. Lives in `src/components/inbox/` (`FloatingMessenger`, `ConversationView`, `ThreadList`) plus `src/hooks/useInbox.ts`. One ongoing 1:1 thread per student with the admin (Joshua). Supports PDF attachments. Gated by `has_chat_access`.\r\n- **Practice surfaces** — Drill and Wrong Answer Journal (WAJ) are real product surfaces, not launchers. Core logic in `src/lib/`: `adaptiveEngine`, `drillIntelligence`, `achievementEngine`, `gamification`, `questionLoader`, `streakSystem`, `wajService`.\r\n- **Admin** — `AdminDashboard` + `admin-manage-users` edge function. Must be desktop AND mobile responsive. Needs granular per-feature toggle control.\r\n- **UI primitives** — shadcn/ui (Radix + `class-variance-authority`) in `src/components/ui/`. Icons from `lucide-react` and `@tabler/icons-react`. Framer Motion for animation. Spline for 3D.\r\n- **Styling** — Tailwind 3.4 over HSL custom properties in `src/index.css`, surfaced as tokens in `tailwind.config.ts`. `DESIGN.md` is the visual authority; `.impeccable/design.json` is its machine-readable sidecar. Prefer tokens over ad-hoc hex values, and never add a token name whose CSS variable is not defined - three such names (`accent-bronze`, `accent-warm`, `glow-bronze`) shipped broken for five months and silently removed the focus ring from every shared Button.\r\n\r\n## Conventions\r\n\r\n- **Git workflow:** Always commit and push directly to `main`. Never create feature branches or pull requests unless explicitly asked. After completing any code change: stage relevant files, commit with a descriptive message, and push to `main`.\r\n- **Push via `.claude/safe-push.sh`, not raw `git push`.** Lovable auto-commits to `main` mid-session; a raw push can get rejected or race a concurrent commit. The script fetches, merges origin/main if it diverged (never rebases, never force-pushes), then pushes.\r\n- **Never roll `main` back.** Do not use Lovable\'s "Revert to commit", "revert and resend", or history restore, and never reset or force-push `main`. On 2026-09-27 an accidental "revert and resend" (b998596) silently undid three merged PRs across 121 question-bank files, and students saw the wrong questions again. To undo your own work, `git revert <your SHA>` only. If a Lovable dialog offers a revert, dismiss it; if one fires, stop and tell Joshua before repairing anything.\r\n- Path alias `@/` → `./src/` (set in `vite.config.ts` and `tsconfig.json`).\r\n- TypeScript strict mode is **off** (`strictNullChecks: false`, `noImplicitAny: false`, `noUnusedLocals: false`). Do not flip these without explicit approval — it would cascade into a very large diff.\r\n- Lovable-specific plumbing that must not be removed:\r\n  - `lovable-tagger` Vite plugin injects `data-lov-id` in dev\r\n  - `vite-plugin-pwa` configured with start URL `/foyer`\r\n  - Lovable preview Service Worker guard in `src/main.tsx`\r\n'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script running with cell ID 7\nWall time 1.0 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'const norm = (word: string) => word.replace(/’/g, "\'").replace(/[^\\p{L}\\p{N}\'-]/gu, \'\').toLowerCase();\r\nconst words = (text: string) => text.split(/\\s+/).map(norm).filter(Boolean);\r\n// Articles and grammatical linking words may be omitted; permission and obligation stay in the condition.\r\nconst LEADING = new Set([\'a\', \'an\', \'the\', \'to\', \'will\', \'is\', \'are\', \'be\']);\r\nfunction core(list: string[]) {\r\n  let start = 0;\r\n  while (start < list.length - 1 && LEADING.has(list[start])) start += 1;\r\n  let end = list.length;\r\n  if (end - start > 1 && list[end - 1] === \'to\') end -= 1;\r\n  return list.slice(start, end).join(\' \');\r\n}\r\nconst accepted = (entry: string) => entry.split(\'|\').map(text => core(words(text)));\r\n\r\nexport const hasConditionSpans = (statement: string) => statement in SPANS;\r\nexport function conditionAnswer(statement: string, role: ConditionRole): string {\r\n  const entry = SPANS[statement]?.[role === \'sufficient\' ? 0 : 1] ?? \'\';\r\n  return entry.split(\'|\')[0].trim();\r\n}\r\n/** Tokens match how the screen splits the rule: on whitespace, punctuation left attached. */\r\nexport function spanText(statement: string, span: number[]): string {\r\n  const tokens = statement.split(/\\s+/);\r\n  return tokens.slice(span[0], span[1] + 1).join(\' \').replace(/^[^\\p{L}\\p{N}]+/u, \'\').replace(/[.,;:!?]+$/, \'\');\r\n}\r\nexport function roleOfSpan(statement: string, span: number[]): ConditionRole | null {\r\n  const entry = SPANS[statement];\r\n  if (!entry) return null;\r\n  const tokens = statement.split(/\\s+/);\r\n  const picked = core(tokens.slice(span[0], span[1] + 1).map(norm).filter(Boolean));\r\n  if (accepted(entry[0]).includes(picked)) return \'sufficient\';\r\n  if (accepted(entry[1]).includes(picked)) return \'necessary\';\r\n  return null;\r\n}\r\nexport function checkConditionSpans(statement: string, spans: number[][]): SpanCheck {\r\n  const roles = spans.map(span => roleOfSpan(statement, span));\r\n  if (spans.length < 2) return { correct: false, roles, hint: \'Highlight both conditions. Click the first word of a condition, then its last word.\' };\r\n  if (roles.some(role => !role)) return { correct: false, roles, hint: \'One highlight is not a complete condition. Include every restriction, such as “within 30 days,” and leave the indicator out.\' };\r\n  if (roles[0] === roles[1]) return { correct: false, roles, hint: \'Both highlights are the same condition. The rule has two different ones.\' };\r\n  return { correct: true, roles, hint: \'\' };\r\n}\r\n\r\n/** Test hook: every authored wording must be found in its rule, without overlap. */\r\nexport function spanEntries() { return Object.entries(SPANS); }\r\nexport function locateWording(statement: string, wording: string): [number, number] | null {\r\n  const tokens = statement.split(/\\s+/).map(norm);\r\n  const target = words(wording);\r\n  for (let start = 0; start + target.length <= tokens.length; start += 1) {\r\n    const slice = tokens.slice(start, start + target.length);\r\n    if (slice.every((word, i) => word === target[i])) return [start, start + target.length - 1];\r\n  }\r\n  return null;\r\n}\r\n'}]
